The bridge from the company's own reported figures to the basis consensus uses

  • EBITDA. The company reports EBITDA without adjustments, and it includes the equity-accounted joint-venture income (FY2025 EUR 14.0m) and other operating income (about EUR 11m in FY2025; it includes, for example, a EUR 1.6m US tariff refund in FY2026). Brokers use the company basis: FY2025 EUR 280.3m company against EUR 278m on FactSet's brokers' basis. FactSet's "operating" EBITDA leaves out the joint-venture and other operating income: FY2025 EUR 255m (25.9%); third quarter FY2026 EUR 67.0m against the company's EUR 75.5m. My EBITDA margins are on the company and brokers' basis. Subtract about 2.3 to 2.6 points to compare with FactSet's history line.
  • Brokers not on the company basis. UBS (FY2026 EBITDA EUR 272m, 26.1%) and one restricted broker (EUR 272m on EUR 1,046m) are below the guided 27% floor, so they almost certainly exclude the joint-venture and other operating income. Their EBITDA should not be compared with the EUR 310m FY2027 median. Their EPS can be.
  • EPS. The company makes no adjustments, so reported EPS equals the brokers' basis (FY2025 EUR 0.97 on both). My EPS is reported EPS on 150.6m shares.
  • Pre-tax profit and tax. FactSet's pre-tax profit (FY2025 EUR 174m) excludes joint-venture income, which FactSet adds after tax. Its 23.5% tax rate is measured on that basis. On pre-tax profit including joint ventures (about EUR 188m), the FY2025 rate was about 21.8%. My tax inputs use the second basis, to match EBITDA including joint ventures.
  • Financial result. FactSet's FY2025 "net interest" of EUR -19.7m is not the company's financial result, which was about EUR -13m (interest expense EUR 12.3m, of which EUR 12.2m was paid to SCHOTT AG). My net financial items are on the company basis.
  • Free cash flow. FactSet's historical free cash flow here deducts dividends (FY2025: 180 - 144 - 24 = EUR 11.5m). The company's own FY2025 figure (EUR 36.8m) is operating cash flow less capital spending. Consensus free cash flow (FY2026 to FY2029: EUR 69m, 95m, 125m, 129m) is close to consensus operating cash flow less capital spending, before dividends and leases.
  • Net debt. The company's net debt excludes leases (EUR 106.0m at 30-Jun-2026; EUR 122.2m at 30-Sep-2025). FactSet's quarterly debt reads zero and is not usable. FactSet's snapshot debt of EUR 231m is the gross liability to SCHOTT AG.
  • Dividend dating. FactSet's dividend per share here is dated by the year the dividend is declared for (FY2025 EUR 0.18); the cash is paid in the following fiscal year.

Earnings per share: reported (the brokers' basis), on 150.6m shares, after minorities of about EUR 0.3m to 0.4m. Free cash flow: operating cash flow (after tax, interest, working capital, and after deducting about EUR 9m to 13m a year of joint-venture income that is not received in cash) less capital spending and lease payments, before dividends. On the company's own definition (before lease payments) it is EUR 65m, 62m, 102m, 136m and 158m. Consensus EPS (FactSet median, 11-Nov-2026): EUR 0.98, 1.10, 1.22 and 1.32 for FY2026 to FY2029. Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 3 and Layer 4

Research requests for the next set

Company: SCHOTT Pharma AG & Co. KGaA (1SXP-DE), a German maker of primary packaging for injectable drugs, 77% owned by SCHOTT AG (Carl Zeiss Foundation), fiscal year ending 30 September. It reports two segments: Drug Containment Solutions (glass vials, ampoules and cartridges, increasingly as sterile ready-to-use formats) and Drug Delivery Systems (glass and polymer prefillable syringes).

Request: T-D1

Peers: Stevanato Group (closest listed rival in glass syringes and ready-to-use cartridges, with a greenfield plant in Fishers, Indiana still ramping and a GLP-1 customer volume cut announced 3-Nov-2026); Gerresheimer (rival in vials and syringes, running the "gto" transformation programme, closing Chicago Heights and selling Centor); West Pharmaceutical Services (precedent for margin expansion through high-value mix with full plants and a capital-spending taper); Becton Dickinson Pharmaceutical Systems (prefillable syringe scale leader, adding Neopak capacity at Columbus, Nebraska); GVS (medtech consumables, precedent for margin recovery through footprint consolidation); Nolato (medical pens and components, start-up cost precedent in new plants); SGD Pharma (private bulk glass vial maker, behaviour under falling bulk prices) Operating conditions: a capital-spending peak followed by a taper while new plants are still being validated; industry-wide capacity additions in glass syringes and ready-to-use formats with falling utilisation (2026 to 2030); single-customer volume cuts under take-or-pay contracts; the end of post-pandemic destocking and the return to just-in-time ordering; GLP-1 per-dose price deflation and format shifts between syringes, vials, multi-dose cartridges and oral drugs; a strong Swiss franc cost base; a change of chief executive (May 2026) and finance director (August 2025) Inputs: Is SCHOTT Pharma's expected margin recovery, from a group EBITDA margin of 26.8% in the third quarter of FY2026 (company, 12-Aug-2026) towards its "increasing towards 30%" target for FY2029 (company, December 2025), plausible on strengths it has already shown? What did the company's own FY2020 to FY2022 expansion (EBITDA margin up from 22.5% to 26.7%) rest on, and are those conditions present again? How fast have the Hungarian syringe lines (opened June 2024), the St. Gallen ready-to-use cartridge expansion, the Serbian ampoule hub (opened April 2025) and the Lebanon, Pennsylvania ready-to-use vial expansion (June 2026) actually filled, and what did each cost in start-up expense? Management described the combined Serbian and Hungarian ramp-up costs as a "double-digit million euro" annual drag (third-quarter FY2025 call). Can the Drug Delivery Systems EBITDA margin (29.4% in the third quarter of FY2026, against over 35% a year earlier) recover, given under-used polymer lines at Müllheim and newly incurred production-optimisation costs? What has the efficiency programme confirmed by finance director Reinhard Mayer ("double-digit million euro") delivered so far, and on what timetable? How credible is the finance director's statement that capital spending peaks in FY2027 and then tapers (12-Aug-2026), given that consensus capital spending is EUR 149m for FY2027 against EUR 156m for FY2026 (FactSet, 11-Nov-2026)? What is the record of delivery: medium-term targets cut in December 2025 (from at least 10% growth to 6% to 8%; from low-30s margins to "towards 30%"), FY2025 sales guidance cut in August 2025, FY2026 guidance raised in July 2026 partly on a compensation payment of about EUR 15m (Deutsche Bank, 9-Oct-2026), and capital projects delivered on schedule? Which peers attempted a margin recovery from a capital-spending peak with new plants still ramping (Stevanato's 30% EBITDA margin target, Gerresheimer, West from 2016 to 2021, GVS from FY2022 to FY2025), how long did it take, and what separated success from failure? Focus: Whether SCHOTT Pharma can turn capacity built from FY2022 to FY2027 into margin and cash without help from price, as earlier companies with a similar starting point did or failed to do. Also, how long the conversion from commissioning to full utilisation has taken for each comparable plant.

Request: T-D2

Peers: Stevanato Group (syringe and ready-to-use cartridge rival; the closest example of a single-customer GLP-1 volume shock, November 2026); Gerresheimer (vials and syringes; leverage and restructuring); West Pharmaceutical Services (high-value components; price and mix disclosure); Becton Dickinson (syringe scale pricing); Nipro and SGD Pharma (bulk glass at the commodity end); Nolato and Ypsomed (pens and autoinjectors facing expected device price falls); Sartorius Stedim Biotech (precedent for the dispersion of outcomes through destocking and recovery) Operating conditions: glass syringe utilisation falling from about 100% to about 90% in late 2025 (AptarGroup executive, May 2026), with experts projecting 10% to 20% excess syringe capacity in three to five years; renewal of three-year locked-price contracts; take-or-pay renegotiations; a capital-spending peak in FY2027; currency (US dollar about 30% of sales, net short Swiss franc position of CHF 135m at 30-Sep-2025, no hedge accounting); parent contract renewals in December 2027 Inputs: Which few drivers separate SCHOTT Pharma's next four years from FY2020 to FY2026, and how concentrated are they? Test each. (1) The incremental EBITDA margin on extra revenue: how much growth runs through plants already built, and how much needs new fixed cost? (2) Like-for-like price on glass syringe and ready-to-use vial renewals in 2027 and 2028, after ready-to-use 6ml vial prices fell from EUR 0.75 to EUR 0.35 to 0.40 a unit in 2026 (Gerresheimer executive, 27-Oct-2026) and after Stevanato's freed capacity. (3) Customer concentration: GLP-1 work is a low-teens share of sales, about two-thirds of it with Eli Lilly (Deutsche Bank), and one customer was 10.3% of first-half FY2026 sales (company, 13-May-2026). (4) The capital-spending peak and taper, and whether the US$371m North Carolina syringe plant is committed. (5) Depreciation (nine-month FY2026 EUR 65.5m, up 14%; company). (6) The financial result after the intra-group refinancing (nine-month FY2026 EUR -6.2m; company). (7) A re-pricing of glass tubing by SCHOTT AG when the framework agreement reaches its fixed term in December 2027. How wide is the spread of broker FY2027 and FY2028 estimates (FY2027 EPS from EUR 1.04, UBS, 6-Nov-2026, to EUR 1.16; FactSet), and do some brokers' EBITDA figures exclude equity-accounted joint-venture income (UBS FY2026 EBITDA EUR 272m against the company's guided 27% to 28% margin)? Historically, how dispersed were outcomes for pharma packaging companies through capacity build-outs and customer cuts, and how often were forecasts met that rested on one or two drivers (margin from utilisation; price)? Focus: How wide and how skewed the plausible range of SCHOTT Pharma's FY2027 and first-half FY2028 revenue, EBITDA, EPS and free cash flow is, and whether that range is driven mainly by utilisation, price, one customer or capital spending.

Request: T-I1

Peers: Stevanato Group (guided 2027 organic growth to low single digits on 3-Nov-2026); West Pharmaceutical Services (third-quarter 2026 results and guidance; price above 4 points in the quarter); Gerresheimer (third-quarter 2026 results, gto targets, covenant reset); GVS (raised FY2026 guidance and 2026 to 2028 plan, 24-Sep-2026); Nolato (Medical Solutions growth and margins, 28-Oct-2026); Becton Dickinson (Pharmaceutical Systems commentary); Sartorius Stedim Biotech (consumables recovery) Operating conditions: the first FY2027 guidance on 10-Dec-2026 against a FY2026 base that includes a one-off compensation payment of about EUR 15m in the fourth quarter (Deutsche Bank, 9-Oct-2026) and an inventory write-down of EUR 12.25m in the first half (company); a new chief executive (Christian Mias, from 1-May-2026); medium-term targets of 6% to 8% organic growth for FY2027 to FY2029 and an EBITDA margin "increasing towards 30%"; sector sentiment after Stevanato's 28% share-price fall on 3-Nov-2026 Inputs: What FY2027 guidance is SCHOTT Pharma likely to give on 10-Dec-2026 (organic growth, EBITDA margin, capital spending), and is organic growth measured against the reported FY2026 base that includes the compensation? Deutsche Bank expects a guide below 6% to 8% and models 5.5% (October 2026). What is the company's record of guiding conservatively? FY2026 was guided at 2% to 5% growth and about 27% margin in December 2025 and raised to 5% to 6% and 27% to 28% in July 2026; the company declined to raise after a first-quarter FY2026 beat helped by supplemental orders and extra shifts. Where does consensus sit against likely guidance and against the run-rate (FactSet median, 11-Nov-2026: FY2027 revenue EUR 1,104m, EBITDA EUR 310m, EPS EUR 1.10; FY2028 EUR 1,180m, EUR 342m, EUR 1.22)? How do quarterly comparisons fall across FY2027: a weak second quarter of FY2026 (Drug Delivery Systems organic -9.8%), a strong third quarter (8.3% organic growth but a 26.8% margin) and the compensation in the fourth quarter? What would each outcome need: a raise (Drug Delivery Systems margin recovery, polymer recovery, dollar strength), a meet, or a cut (a GLP-1 customer volume change, syringe price concessions, start-up costs)? What do the latest peer results and guidance imply for GLP-1, biologics and ready-to-use demand, and for pricing in 2027? How are the net breadth of revisions and the buy-rating share moving: the buy share fell 23 points in one month to 46%, while target prices rose 21% over three months (FactSet, 11-Nov-2026)? Focus: How likely SCHOTT Pharma's FY2027 guidance on 10-Dec-2026, and its delivery at the February, May, August and December 2027 results, are to raise, meet or cut, and how likely consensus EPS and free cash flow for FY2027 and FY2028 are to be revised up or down, and why.

Request: T-I2

Peers: Stevanato Group (the sector's GLP-1 syringe bellwether); Gerresheimer (a possible share donor); West Pharmaceutical Services (the high-value component benchmark); Becton Dickinson (syringe scale); Nolato and Ypsomed (pen and autoinjector suppliers); Sartorius Stedim Biotech (consumables recovery narrative) Operating conditions: sector sentiment after Stevanato's GLP-1 customer cut (3-Nov-2026); the obesity-drug debate on oral drugs, generics and per-dose price cuts (Novo Nordisk's lower US prices from January 2027); the BIOSECURE Act list due 18-Dec-2026; low free float and liquidity at SCHOTT Pharma (77% held by SCHOTT AG); Annex 1 and the FDA's August 2026 draft container-closure guidance Inputs: How well known are these dynamics to the sell-side and to investors? (1) The FY2026 compensation payment and write-down: their opposite effects on FY2027 revenue growth and on EBITDA. (2) Equity-accounted joint-venture income (SCHOTT Pharma India; Empha in Italy) inside the company's EBITDA, and the differences between brokers' EBITDA bases. For each: which analysts (Deutsche Bank, Jefferies, UBS, RBC Capital Markets, Stifel, Barclays, Oddo BHF, EQUI.TS, Bernstein, Morgan Stanley) and which investor commentators discuss it, since when, with what view and how prominently? Focus: Which of these dynamics are widely priced into the narrative on SCHOTT Pharma, which only one or two analysts discuss, and which no one discusses, in particular the capital-spending peak, the basis differences in EBITDA and tax, and syringe price pressure after Stevanato's cut.

Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

Written 13 November 2026, 07:21 to 07:44 UK, Fable, ricky login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
SCHOTT Pharma (1SXP-DE): third and fourth sets of questions

The third set: how predictable the financial forecasts are over the next 18 months. The fourth set: how far our assessment is already reflected in company guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative.

Written 2026-11-13 for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo. Share price EUR 21.15 (the model, 11-Nov-26; EUR 21.40 on the dashboard). Fiscal year ends 30 September. FY2026 results and the first FY2027 guidance come on 10-Dec-2026. EBITDA is on the company basis throughout (no adjustments, joint-venture income included), which is also the brokers' basis, unless I say otherwise. "DCS" is Drug Containment Solutions (vials, ampoules, cartridges); "DDS" is Drug Delivery Systems (glass and polymer prefillable syringes).

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Notes for the Consumer before the answers

  1. Where I stand on the forecasts of record

I keep every profit-and-loss input of the second set. My base case is therefore the model's: revenue growth of 5.5% in FY2027 (about 7% underlying) and 6% to 6.5% a year after; an EBITDA margin of 27.5% (FY2026), 27.9%, 28.4%, 28.9% and 29.3%; and EPS of EUR 0.97 (FY2026), 1.05, 1.15, 1.28 and 1.40, against consensus of EUR 0.98, 1.10, 1.22 and 1.32. I change one cash-flow input, working capital, and only its basis (note 3). Three figures in the model's one-page summary must not be quoted as they stand; note 2 explains each.

  1. The three gaps between the second set's cross-check and the model

FY2026 EPS: the cross-check's EUR 0.97 against the model's EUR 0.89. EUR 0.97 is right. EUR 0.89 is the product of two bases being joined inside one year. No input is wrong.

  • The model's FY2026 pre-tax profit (EUR 189.6m) is exactly the second set's own arithmetic: EBITDA 286.6, less depreciation 88.5, less net financial items 8.5. The whole gap sits between pre-tax profit and net income. The model shows net income of EUR 133.7m, an implied tax rate of 29.5% against the input of 23.0%. That is EUR 12.3m of net income, or EUR 0.08 a share. From FY2027 the model applies the input rate exactly (FY2027: 203.2 at 22.5% tax gives 157.5), so the fault is confined to the one year that joins three reported quarters to one forecast quarter.
  • The cause, as far as the summary lets me trace it. The model takes the three reported quarters from FactSet's standardised lines. FactSet's EBITDA for the nine months is EUR 185.5m, against the company's EUR 205.3m, because FactSet leaves out about EUR 20m of joint-venture and other operating income. The margin input for the year (27.5%) is on the company basis. The model's own phasing note shows the result: it raised the fourth-quarter margin by 7.1 points, to 37.1% (EBITDA of about EUR 101m), to make the year add up. Having forced the year together in that way, it arrives at a net income that leaves only about EUR 31m for the fourth quarter (133.7 less the EUR 103.0m already reported for the nine months). That is not a coherent quarter: it is the quarter that contains about EUR 15m of compensation at close to a 100% margin.
  • The same year on one basis. Nine-month net income, reported: EUR 103.0m. Fourth quarter: EBITDA about EUR 81.5m (30.0% of about EUR 272m of revenue), depreciation about EUR 23m, financial result about EUR -2.3m, pre-tax profit about EUR 56m, tax at 23%, net income about EUR 43m. The year: about EUR 146m and EUR 0.97 a share. Consensus is EUR 0.98.
  • What follows for the memo. Do not quote the model's FY2026 EPS (EUR 0.89), its FY2026 EPS growth (-9.0%), its FY2027 EPS growth (+17.8%; the true figure is about +8%, from EUR 0.97 to EUR 1.05), or its FY2026 lines against consensus (-9.4% on EPS; the true figure is about -1%). The model's step in EBITDA from FY2025 (EUR 255.3m, FactSet's basis) to FY2026 (EUR 286.6m, company basis) is also a change of basis, not growth: on the company basis it is EUR 280.3m to EUR 286.6m, +2.2%. The return at the guideline fair multiple is not affected, because it uses two-year-forward EPS, which lies wholly in the forecast years.

FY2026 free cash flow: the cross-check's EUR 58m against the model's EUR 138.3m. EUR 58m is right. Here an input is on the wrong basis for the model, and I change it.

  • The model builds equity free cash flow as net income, plus depreciation, less capital spending, less lease payments, less the increase in working capital. Its working capital at 30-Sep-2025 is FactSet's figure of EUR 287m (receivables plus inventories less payables), which is 29.1% of revenue. The second set's input of 21.0% is on the company's own, narrower definition (20.2% at 30-Sep-2025, about EUR 199m). The model therefore sees working capital fall from EUR 287m to EUR 219m (21.0% of EUR 1,042m) and books a release of EUR 68m that will not happen. The arithmetic reproduces the model's figure within rounding: 133.7 + 88.5 - 145.0 - 7.5 + 68.2 = 137.9. The second set intended an increase of about EUR 20m.
  • After the change in note 3 the model should show FY2026 free cash flow of about EUR 50m. What then separates it from EUR 58m is the understated net income (about EUR 12m too low) less the joint-venture income not received in cash (about EUR 9m, which the model does not deduct).
  • The second set's EUR 58m (EUR 65m before lease payments) fits what is already reported: nine-month free cash flow of EUR 58.9m, and a fourth quarter in which back-loaded capital spending of about EUR 62m roughly matches operating cash flow.

FY2027 free cash flow: the cross-check's EUR 54m against the model's EUR 65.2m. EUR 54m is the better figure. No input is wrong; the model has no line for what causes the gap.

  • The model: 157.5 + 96.0 - 165.0 - 8.0 - 15.3 = 65.2. The second set deducts about EUR 11m more, for joint-venture income that is inside EBITDA and net income but is not received in cash (joint-venture income of about EUR 16.5m, less dividends received). The same EUR 11m to 13m separates the two in every later year (FY2028: 94 against 104.9; FY2029: 127 against 139.1; FY2030: 149 against 162.6).
  • For the memo, use about EUR 54m for FY2027 (EUR 62m on the company's own definition, before lease payments). The evidence on how much cash the joint ventures actually pay to SCHOTT Pharma is thin; it is one of my research requests.

Net debt. Do not quote the model's net cash and net debt levels. FactSet's opening net debt (EUR 57.3m at 30-Sep-2025) compares with the company's EUR 122.2m (excluding leases of about EUR 25m). FactSet's year-end debt of EUR 83.0m omits most of the EUR 220m owed to SCHOTT AG's cash pool, and its quarterly debt reads zero. Use the second set's company-basis path: net debt of about EUR 104m (September 2026), EUR 77m, EUR 12m, then net cash of about EUR 83m (FY2029) and EUR 197m (FY2030). This does not touch EPS, because net financial items are a direct input.

A request to the Financial Analyst. The Financial Analyst should do three things in the model for this stock. First, put FY2026 on one basis: either restate the three reported quarters on the company basis (EBITDA of EUR 205.3m for the nine months, including joint-venture and other operating income), or tie FY2026 net income to the EUR 103.0m reported for the nine months plus the forecast quarter, so that the year comes to about EUR 146m and EUR 0.97 a share. Second, add a line for joint-venture income that is included in EBITDA but not received in cash (about EUR 9m in FY2026, rising to about EUR 13m in FY2030), and deduct it in the cash flow. Third, show net debt on the company's basis (EUR 122.2m at 30-Sep-2025 and EUR 106.0m at 30-Jun-2026, excluding leases).

  1. The one input I change, and two I considered and left

  • Changed: working capital (% of revenue), to 29.4%, 29.3%, 29.1%, 28.8% and 28.6% for FY2026 to FY2030. This is the second set's own path, restated on the model's (FactSet's) definition. It keeps the second set's increases in euros (about EUR 19m, 16m, 19m, 18m and 19m). It is a change of basis, not of view. It removes the false EUR 68m release from FY2026 and about EUR 88m from the model's net cash in every year from FY2026.
  • Considered and left: the FY2027 EBITDA margin (27.9%). The underlying margin fell during FY2026 (D-01, point 1), and FY2027 needs about 160 basis points more than the second half's rate. Deutsche Bank forecasts a flat margin. But Deutsche Bank's flat margin on its higher revenue gives the same EUR 306m of EBITDA as our 27.9% on ours. I keep the input and carry the risk in the cone (D-04).
  • Considered and left: FY2027 capital spending (EUR 165m). Management said on 12-Aug-2026 that FY2027 investment would be "slightly above" FY2026, which points to EUR 150m to 160m if FY2026 lands near EUR 140m to 145m. The difference is within the noise.

Co-movement of 1SXP-DE with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-11-12 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: SCHOTT Pharma AG & Co. KGaA (1SXP-DE), a German maker of primary packaging for injectable drugs, 77% owned by SCHOTT AG (Carl Zeiss Foundation), fiscal year ending 30 September; two segments: Drug Containment Solutions (glass vials, ampoules and cartridges, increasingly as sterile ready-to-use formats) and Drug Delivery Systems (glass and polymer prefillable syringes)

Request: OWN-1

Question: What are investors and sell-side analysts saying about SCHOTT Pharma AG & Co. KGaA (1SXP-DE) since Stevanato Group cut its 2027 growth outlook on 3-Nov-2026, and how are investors positioned in the stock? Cover four things. (a) Investor commentary of every kind: fund letters and manager interviews, X/Twitter and other social media, investor forums, and the questions investors and analysts asked on SCHOTT Pharma's results calls in 2026 and at broker conferences. (b) Positioning: the shareholder register outside SCHOTT AG's 77% holding, recent changes in the largest institutional holdings, short interest and stock on loan, trading volumes, index membership and the free float (18% per Deutsche Börse; company presentation, 30-Jul-2026). (c) Every broker note on SCHOTT Pharma published since 1-Oct-2026, including Deutsche Bank's downgrade to Hold (9-Oct-2026), RBC Capital Markets (4-Nov-2026), UBS (6-Nov-2026) and any previews of the FY2026 results and first FY2027 guidance due on 10-Dec-2026: the rating, the target price, the valuation method and target multiple, what each broker expects the FY2027 guidance to be, and what each says would change its view. (d) For the peers Stevanato Group, Gerresheimer, West Pharmaceutical Services, Ypsomed and Nolato: the direction of estimate and target-price revisions and the share-price reaction since their latest results. Say which arguments are widely held, which are made by only one or two houses or commentators, and which bullish or bearish arguments nobody is making; date every item and credit each view to its own source.

Request: OWN-2

Question: At what valuation multiples have SCHOTT Pharma AG & Co. KGaA (1SXP-DE) and its closest peers traded in each phase of their earnings cycle, and what evidence did it take for the multiple to re-rate? For SCHOTT Pharma since its listing in September 2023, and for West Pharmaceutical Services (2015 to 2026), Stevanato Group (2021 to 2026), Gerresheimer (2018 to 2026), Ypsomed and Sartorius Stedim Biotech, give the forward price-to-earnings and EV/EBITDA multiples at each turn: guidance cuts, troughs, the first evidence of margin recovery, periods of repeated guidance raises, capital-spending peaks and the first year in which free cash flow rose sharply. Say how long each re-rating or de-rating took and what specific results or announcements triggered it. What target multiples do brokers apply to SCHOTT Pharma today and how do they justify them (for example Deutsche Bank's target multiple of 22x price-to-earnings in October 2026 against 15x in February 2026, and Jefferies' discounted cash flow)? How large a discount, if any, do brokers and investors apply for SCHOTT AG's 77% holding, the KGaA structure and the low free float? What happened to the valuation multiple and the trading liquidity of comparable German listed subsidiaries when the parent placed shares or announced a plan for its stake (for example Siemens Healthineers and Carl Zeiss Meditec), and has SCHOTT AG or the Carl Zeiss Foundation said anything in 2026 about its holding in SCHOTT Pharma?

Request: OWN-3

Question: How much of SCHOTT Pharma AG & Co. KGaA's (1SXP-DE) reported profit turns into cash, and how firm is the fall in capital spending that management expects after FY2027? Cover five things. (a) The equity-accounted joint ventures (SCHOTT Pharma India, formerly SCHOTT Poonawalla, 50% owned, in which TPG bought a 35% stake; and Empha in Italy): the income from them included in SCHOTT Pharma's EBITDA in each year since FY2021, the dividends SCHOTT Pharma actually received from them in each year, their own investment plans and any stated policy on distributions. (b) Working capital: the company's own definition and ratio (20.2% of revenue at 30-Sep-2025 and 21.8% at 30-Jun-2026) against the definitions and forecasts brokers use, its components (inventories, trade receivables, contract assets, customer prepayments and contract liabilities), and what management has said about its direction as the share of ready-to-use products grows. (c) Capital spending: every management statement in 2025 and 2026 on the FY2027 peak and the level afterwards (the finance director said on 12-Aug-2026 that spending peaks in FY2027 and tapers thereafter), the split between maintenance and growth spending, the projects still to be paid for (the ready-to-use cartridge plant in Hungary, polymer syringe capacity at Müllheim, India) and the latest stated status of the US$371m prefillable syringe plant planned for Wilson, North Carolina. (d) What SCHOTT Pharma, SCHOTT AG and brokers have said about the use of cash once capital spending falls: the dividend policy of 10% to 20% of earnings, repayment of borrowings from SCHOTT AG's cash pool, or new projects. (e) How free cash flow, capital intensity and return on capital developed at West Pharmaceutical Services, Stevanato Group and Gerresheimer in the two to three years after their own capital-spending peaks, and what brokers forecast for SCHOTT Pharma's capital spending and free cash flow in FY2028 and FY2029.
